      n          44.1.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site of a building or other construction\\\
      n                 work that all working areas in a free tunnel are provided with ventilation system as\\n\
      \                 approved by the DG and the fresh air supply in such tunnel is not less than 6m3/min\\n \
      \                      for each building worker employed underground in such tunnel and the free air flow\\\
      n                movement inside such tunnel is not less than 9m/min.\\n\\n\\n          44.1.2   The oxygen\
      \ level shall not be less than 19.5% in the working environment.\\n\\n\\n          44.2      Illumination\\\
      n\\n          44.2.1   The contractor shall take every effort to illuminate the work site as per the Employer’s\\\
      n                   requirement illustrated in general instruction BMRC/SHE/CEO/011.\\n\\n          44.2.2\
      \   The contractor shall conduct a monthly illumination monitoring by lux meter for\\n                   \
      \      all the locations and the report shall be sent to the Employer within 7th of the\\n               \
      \   next month and the same shall be reviewed during the monthly SHE committee\\n                  meeting.\\\
      n\\n\\n          45.0     Radiation\\n\\n\\n          45.1    The use of radioactive substances and radiating\
      \ apparatus shall comply with the Govt.\\n                    regulatory requirements and all subsidiary legislation\\\
      n\\n\\n          45.2     Operations involving ionising radiation shall only be carried out after having been\\\
      n                  reviewed without objection by the Employers representative and shall be carried out in\\\
      n                  accordance with a method statement.\\n\\n\\n          45.3     each area containing irradiated\
      \ apparatus shall have warning notices and barriers, as\\n                    required by the Regulations,\
      \ conspicuously posted at or near the area.\\n\\n\\n          45.4     Radioactive substances will be stored,\
      \ used or disposed shall be strictly in accordance\\n                     with the Govt. Enactments.\\n\\\
      n\\n          45.5    The contractor shall ensure that all site personnel and members of the public are not\\\
      n                 exposed to radiation.\\n\\n\\n          46.0     Welfare measures for workers\\n\\n\\n \
      \         46.1      Latrine and Urinal Accommodation\\n\\n\\n          46.1.1   The contractor shall provide\
      \ one latrine seat for every 20 workers up to 100\\n                 workers and thereafter one for every\
      \ additional 50 workers. In addition one\\n                    urinal accommodation shall be provided for\
      \ every 100 workers.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.2   When women are employed, separate latrine and urinals accommodation\
      \ shall be\\n                   provided on the same scale as mentioned above.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.3  \
      \  Latrine and urinals shall be provided as per Section 33 of BOCWA and maintained as\\n                 \
      \  per Rule 243 of BOCWR and shall also comply with the requirements of public health\\n                 \
      \     authorities\\n\\n\\n          46.1.4   Moving sites\\n\\n\\n          46.1.4.1  In case of works like\
      \ track laying, the zone of work is constantly moving at elevated\\n                      level or at underground\
      \ level. In such cases mobile toilets with proper facility to drain\\n                    the sullage shall\
      \ be provided at reasonably accessible distance.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.5    In case if the contractor fail\
      \ to provide required number of urinals and latrines\\n                  or fail to maintain it as per the\
      \ requirements of Public Health laws, the Employer\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.\

      : \" \\n45.1 \\nThe use of radioactive substances and radiating apparatus shall comply with the Govt. \\nregulatory\
      \ requirements and all subsidiary legislation \\n \\n45.2 \\nOperations involving ionising radiation shall\
      \ only be carried out after having been \\nreviewed without objection by the Employers representative and\
      \ shall be carried out in \\naccordance with a method statement. \\n \\n45.3 \\neach area containing irradiated\
      \ apparatus shall have warning notices and barriers, as \\nrequired by the Regulations, conspicuously posted\
      \ at or near the area. \\n \\n45.4 \\nRadioactive substances will be stored, used or disposed shall be strictly\
      \ in accordance \\nwith the Govt. Enactments. \\n \\n45.5 \\nThe contractor shall ensure that all site personnel\
      \ and members of the public are not \\nexposed to radiation.   \\n \\n46.0 \\nWelfare measures for workers\
      \ \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55965423583984,\n        497.55914306640625,\n     \
      \   515.887451171875,\n        665.0916137695312\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 6,\n      \"text\": \"\
      \ \\n46.1 \\nLatrine and Urinal Accommodation \\n \\n46.1.1 \\nThe contractor shall provide one latrine seat\
      \ for every 20 workers up to 100 \\nworkers and thereafter one for every additional 50 workers. In addition\
      \ one \\nurinal accommodation shall be provided for every 100 workers. \\n \\n46.1.2 \\nWhen women are employed,\
      \ separate latrine and urinals accommodation shall be \\nprovided on the same scale as mentioned above. \\\
      n \\n46.1.3 \\nLatrine and urinals shall be provided as per Section 33 of BOCWA and maintained as \\nper Rule\
      \ 243 of BOCWR and shall also comply with the requirements of public health \\nauthorities \\n\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        669.9990844726562,\n        188.8888702392578,\n\
      \        692.9317016601562\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 7,\n      \"text\": \" \\n46.1.4 \\nMoving sites\
      \ \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        697.8390502929688,\n       \
      \ 515.8886108398438,\n        784.1316528320312\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 8,\n      \"text\": \"\
      \ \\n46.1.4.1 In case of works like track laying, the zone of work is constantly moving at elevated \\nlevel\
      \ or at underground level. In such cases mobile toilets with proper facility to drain \\nthe sullage shall\
      \ be provided at reasonably accessible distance. \\n \\n46.1.5 \\nIn case if the contractor fail to provide\
      \ required number of urinals and latrines \\nor fail to maintain it as per the requirements of Public Health\
      \ laws, the Employer \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        39.599998474121094,\n        24.479991912841797,\n\
      \        63.57600021362305,\n        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 9,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"255\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

      \   56.1    BMRC has built an image of safety conscious organisation meticulously over a period\\n       \

      \     reputation of BMRC. Most of the accidents are avoidable and caused preliminary due\\n              to\
      \ contractors’ negligence. Hence BMRC shall recover the cost of damages from the\\n             contractors\
      \ for every reportable incident (fatality / injury).\\n\\n\\n   56.2      In addition every BMRC work  site\
      \  is exposed to public scrutiny as the work  is\\n            executed just on the right-of-way. Any unsafe\

      \ non-voluntary compliance of\\n             contractors to the condition of contract on SHE and project SHE\
      \ manual, BMRC has\\n           been forced to establish safety-enforcing organisation. The cost of established\
      \ such\\n             organisation is to be recovered from contractors for all observed safety violations\
      \ at\\n               sites.\\n\\n\\n   56.3    The following table indicates the Safety, Health and Environment\

      n                            Canteen not provided (clause 46.2)\\n\\n                           Food stuff\
      \ not served on no loss no profit basis\\n                                 (clause 46.3)\\n\\n           \
      \                 Creche not provided (clause 46.6)\\n\\n                         Non adherence of Labour\
      \ welfare provisions of\\n                    BOCWA (clause 3.3.1.2)\\n\\n                               \
      \       Fail  to  register establishment and  display the\\n                                     registration\
      \   certificate   at  workplace  (clause\\n                                    3.3.1.2)\\n\\n            \
      \                Absence of workers register and records (clause\\n                                    3.3.1.2)\\\
      n\\n                            Absence of muster roll and wages register (clause\\n                     \
      \               3.3.1.2)\\n\\n                                      Fail  to  display an  abstract  of BOCWA\
      \ and\\n                    BOCWR (clause 3.3.1.2)\\n\\n         28.  Environmental     Tyre wash facility\
      \ not provided (clause 47.12)      Rs.10,000 per single\\n           Management       Spillage from vehicles\
      \ not arrest (clause 48.9)       violation Compounded\\n                                                 \
      \                                           to a maximum of\\n                                      Air monitoring\
      \ not practiced (clause 47.17)\\n                                                                        \
      \    Rs.50,000 at any single\\n                              Noise monitoring not practiced (clause 43.2.1)\
      \      instance\\n                          The values of air monitoring and noise monitoring\\n         \
      \                        not with in acceptable limits (clause 47.17, 43.2.1)\\n\\n                      \
      \        Dust  control measures  at  sites  not  practiced\\n                                 (clause 47.13)\\\
      n\\n                              Improper disposal of debris / residues\\n\\n                         Non\
      \ compliance of clause 53.0 & 54.0\\n\\n\\n          56.4     Without limiting to the unsafe acts and or conditions\
      \ mentioned above in clause 56.3\\n                  the Employer shall have the right to deduct charges for\
      \ any other unsafe act and or\\n                  condition depending upon the gravity of the situation on\
      \ a case-to-case basis. The\\n                 charges shall be in comparison with that of the similar offence\
      \ indicated in clause\\n                     56.3.\\n\\n\\n          57.0     Stoppage of work\\n\\n\\n  \
      \        57.1    The Employer shall have the right to stop the work at his sole discretion, if in his opinion\
      \ the\\n                 work is being carried out in such a way that  it may cause accidents and endanger\
      \ the\\n                    safety of the persons and / or property, and / or equipments. In such cases, the\
      \ contractor\\n                      shall be informed in writing about the nature of hazards and possible\
      \ injury / accident.\\n\\n\\n          57.2    The contractor shall not proceed with the work until he has\
      \ complied with each direction to\\n                    the satisfaction of Employer.\\n\\n          57.3\
      \    The Contractor shall not be entitled for any damages / compensation for stoppage of work,\\n        \
      \        due to safety reasons and the period of such stoppage of work shall not be taken as an\\n       \
      \            extension of time for Completion of the Facilities and will not be the ground for waiver of\\\
      n                    levy of liquidated damages.\\n\\n\\n          58.0    Awards\\n\\n                The\
      \ following categories will be considered for awards as per the scheme in practice of\\n                 Employer\\\
      n\\n                                 i)    For every safe million man hour working without any reportable\
      \ incidents\\n\\n                                    ii)    Zero fatality contracts\\n\\n                \
      \                     iii)   100% adherence to voluntary reporting of all accidents throughout the currency\
      \ of\\n                           contract\\n\\n                          iv)    Safest project team of the\
      \ year.\\n\\n                      v)    Best SHE team of the year.\\n\\n                          vi)   \
      \ Safest Contractor of the year.\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September\
      \ 2026     Section Page 83 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        50.15998077392578,\n\

      \ \\n56.4 \\nWithout limiting to the unsafe acts and or conditions mentioned above in clause 56.3 \\nthe Employer\
      \ shall have the right to deduct charges for any other unsafe act and or \\ncondition depending upon the gravity\
      \ of the situation on a case-to-case basis. The \\ncharges shall be in comparison with that of the similar\
      \ offence indicated in clause \\n56.3. \\n \\n57.0  \\nStoppage of work  \\n \\n57.1 \\nThe Employer shall\
      \ have the right to stop the work at his sole discretion, if in his opinion the \\nwork is being carried out\
      \ in such a way that it may cause accidents and endanger the \\nsafety of the persons and / or property, and\
      \ / or equipments. In such cases, the contractor \\nshall be informed in writing about the nature of hazards\
      \ and possible injury / accident. \\n \\n57.2 \\nThe contractor shall not proceed with the work until he has\
      \ complied with each direction to \\nthe satisfaction of Employer. \\n57.3 \\nThe Contractor shall not be\
      \ entitled for any damages / compensation for stoppage of work, \\ndue to safety reasons and the period of\
      \ such stoppage of work shall not be taken as an \\nextension of time for Completion of the Facilities and\
      \ will not be the ground for waiver of \\nlevy of liquidated damages. \\n \\n58.0 \\nAwards \\nThe following\
      \ categories will be considered for awards as per the scheme in practice of \\nEmployer \\ni) \\nFor every\
      \ safe million man hour working without any reportable incidents \\nii) \\nZero fatality contracts \\niii)\
      \ \\n100% adherence to voluntary reporting of all accidents throughout the currency of \\ncontract \\niv)\
      \ \\nSafest project team of the year. \\nv) \\nBest SHE team of the year. \\nvi) \\nSafest Contractor of the\

  - id: J045457175f78f13f
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_appendices_instructions_forms.md
    okf_sha256: 4bf2dd6ca29bbff0464ffc5fecbea9e86db21baafe4df5bda2628f3ddd97b36f
    heading: Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Appendices, General Instructions and Forms
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52
    pdf_pages:
    - 95
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/appendix_4_workplace_policy/workplace_policy.json
    json_sha256: 96a8da18726a9d4c7a2150f8e27855b32f1e8ccd7552643932a26c9a79a954a4
    json_pointer: /introduction_quote_and_attribution
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 95,\n      \"text\": \"BANGALORE METRO RAIL CORPORATION\
      \ LTD.\\n\\n\\n\\n                                APPENDIX NO.: 4\\n\\n\\n\\n        WORKPLACE POLICY ON HIV/AIDS\
      \ PREVENTION & CONTROL FOR WORKMEN\\n                        ENGAGED BY CONTRACTORS\\n\\n             “Being\
      \ mobile in and of itself is not a risk factor for HIV infection.  It is the situations\\n           encountered\
      \ and the behaviours possibly engaged in during mobility or migration that\\n                           increase\
      \ vulnerability and risk regarding HIV / AIDS.”\\n\\n               UNAIDS, Technical update on ‘Population,\
      \ Mobility and AIDS’, February 2001, p.5\\n\\n        Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation (BMRC) recognizes HIV\
      \  / AIDS as a developmental\\n         challenge and realizes the need to respond to  it by implementing\
      \ regular HIV  / AIDS\\n         prevention programmes and  creating a  non-discriminatory work environment\

    json_pointer: /instructions/8/fragments/0
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"pdf_page\": 107,\n  \"text\": \"BANGALORE METRO RAIL CORPORATION LTD.\\n\\n\\n\\n           \
      \                 General Instruction: BMRC/SHE/CEO/009\\n\\n\\n\\n         Minimum Requirements of SHE Communication\
      \ Posters / Signages / Video\\n\\n\\n          1.     For the purpose of Minimum requirements of SHE Communication\
      \ Posters  / Signages  /\\n              Video the contracts are categorized into the following groups:\\\
      n\\n\\n                           Contract Value (Initial awarded value of contract)              Group\\\
      n\\n\\n                                        Upto 25 Cr                          A\\n\\n\\n            \
      \                           Upto 100 Cr                         B\\n\\n\\n                               \
      \        Upto 250 Cr                       C\\n\\n\\n                                  More than 250 Cr  \
      \                    D\\n\\n\\n          2.     Every contractor falling into the above groups shall prepare\
      \ a SHE Communication Plan as a\\n                 part of site specific SHE Plan and shall include the following\
      \ minimum requirement of Posters /\\n              Signages / Video as applicable. In case readymade posters\
      \ are available in any of the category\\n               from National Safety Council, Loss Prevention Association\
      \ of India or any other safety related\\n                organisations they may procure the same and display\
      \ it. In case the same is not available then\\n                the contractors’ shall make necessary arrangements\
      \ to get the posters designed and printed\\n             on their own.\\n\\n\\n                    All the\
      \ above are to be detailed in the Site SHE Plan and get an approval from the Employer\\n                before\
      \ displaying the posters.\\n\\n\\n                                  Table No.: 1 - Minimum No. of Posters\\\
      n\\n\\n                                   Minimum           No. of Posters / Signage / Video\\n          \
      \                                No. of\\n                                              Group A  Group B \
      \ Group C  Group D\\n          Sl.No    SHE Poster Title      concepts\\n                                \
      \                      Contract   Contract   Contract   Contract\\n                                      \
      \            in each\\n                                                                 title\\n\\n      \
      \        1.    Safety Culture                5      Each 10   Each 50   Each 75   Each 100\\n\\n         \
      \     2.    Daily Safety Oath         1 English    Each 100  Each 200  Each 500 Each 1000\\n             \
      \                      &1 Hindi\\n\\n              3.    Mandatory PPE Usage\\n\\n               a)  Signages\
      \ to display the    2 types of    Each 25   Each 50   Each 75   Each 200\\n               messages like PPE\
      \         sizes made\\n              ZONE, NO PPE ZONE,    up of metal\\n            HARD HAT AREA etc.  \
      \   sheet to be\\n                                      mounted at\\n                                    \
      \                 different\\n                                                 locations\\n\\n           \
      \   b)  Helmet                     5      Each 25   Each 50   Each 75   Each 200\"\n}"

      \  \"text\": \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157                                        Financial\
      \ Document \\n \\n \\n \\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                            September 2026\
      \ \\nSection Page 3 of 7 \\n \\nPricing Document-Preamble \\n1. The Pricing Document-Bill of Quantities (BOQ)\
      \ & Abstract to Bill of Quantities (BOQ) \\nshall be read in conjunction with the entire Tender and Contract\
      \ Document including \\nInstructions to Tenderers, General Conditions of Contract (including ‘Contract Data’),\
      \ \\nSpecial Conditions of Contract, Notice Inviting Tender, Technical Specifications, \\nEmployer’s Requirements,\
      \ Scope of work,  Schedule, Attachments, Appendix, Annexure, \\nCorrigendum and Addendums. \\n2. The quantities\
      \ given in the Price document - “Bill of Quantities” are estimated \\n(provisional). The basis of payment\
      \ will be the actual quantities of work ordered and \\ncarried out, as measured by the Contractor and verified\
      \ by the Engineer and valued at \\nthe rates in the accepted priced “Price Bid – Summary of Bill of Quantities”.\
      \ \\n3. Tenderer shall quote in the Price Bid (.xls format) sheet only in accordance with the ITT \\nclause\
      \ 10. Tenderer is advised to quote their percentage rate in two decimals for \\nSchedule-A. Tenderer has to\
      \ select option “At Par / Excess / Less” and enter percentage \\nrate in figures on estimated cost to Schedule-A,\
      \ for Schedule-B tenderer is required to \\nselect percentage option as ‘At Par’ only and for Schedule-C tenderer\
      \ shall offer Credit \\nto BMRCL on Salvage of Materials, in ‘Lump sum’ amount. \\n4. The Lump sum amount\
      \ indicated for Schedule C will be recovered from the running \\naccount bills proportionately. In case of\
      \ any variation in Quantities (increase/decrease), \\nthe value of credit to BMRCL will be increased/decreased\
      \ according to the value of work \\ndone from the ‘on account’ bills on pro-rata basis. \\n5. The Contractor\
      \ should note that the credit to BMRCL on salvage materials should be in \\nreference to the quoted price\
      \ and not on the estimate cost put to tender. \\n6. The ‘Tender Price’ or ‘Quoted Price’ includes all applicable\
      \ taxes, duties, cess, royalties, \\nand statutory levies as per the laws in force as on last day of the month\
      \ previous to the \\nmonth of invitation of this tender, except Goods and Services Tax (GST). \\n7. The method\
      \ of measurement of completed work for payment shall be in accordance with \\nthe requirements as stated in\
      \ the Technical Specifications and as given in the Price \\ndocument- Bill of Quantities or elsewhere in the\
      \ tender documents. \\n8. The quoted price is for completed and finished items of work and complete in all\
      \ respects \\ncomplying with the provisions of the Contract. It will be deemed to have included all \\nconstructional\
      \ plant, machinery tools, fuel, oil, consumables, electric power, water, \\ntransportation, disposal of debris\
      \ at BBMP/GBA identified C&D waste management \\nfacility (dismantled/demolished material), all leads & lifts,\
      \ all temporary works & false \\nworks, fencing, watering, lighting, maintenance, night working and inspection\
      \ facilities. \\n9. The quoted price will be deemed to have included all the safety measures at work sites/\
      \ \\ncasting yard, safety measures for workmen & road users, safety of the public and \\nprotection of adjoining\
      \ land & building while working. \\n10. The quoted price will be deemed to have included establishment & overhead\
      \ charges, \\ncontractor’s profit, supervision, other charges together with all general risks, liabilities\
      \ \\nand obligations set out or implied in the contract and including remedy of any defects \\nduring the\
      \ Defect Liability Period.  \\n11. The quoted price will be deemed to have included accommodations for staff\
      \ & workmen, \\nall insurance costs for labour & works, the payment of all wages, salaries, fees if any, \\\
      ncharges payable to various departments for obtaining necessary approvals if any. \\n305\\n\",\n  \"text_blocks\"\

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol1/secC#Clause 3.0 — Qualification Criteria|Clause 3.0 — Qualification Criteria]]

>       "pdf_page": 32,
>       "text": "3.0 \nQUALIFICATION CRITERIA \n \nPursuant to clause 2.0, tenderers shall meet the qualification criteria stipulated in the succeeding paragraphs in order to qualify. \n3.1 WORK EXPERIENCE & FINANCIAL STANDING: \n3.1. A. WORK EXPERIENCE \nSl. No \nCriteria Requirement \nDocument Submission \nRequirement \n3.1.A.(i) \nTenderer as a Prime Contractor (Single entity or Joint Venture member) during last 07(seven) \nyears ending as on last day of the month previous to the month of invitation of this tender, \nshould have completed/ **substantially completed One work of *Similar nature, where the \nvalue of the work is equal to or exceed ₹ 3.79 Crores.*** \nAttachment-5 & Attachment-\n6 to Letter of Technical Bid \nOR \n3.1.A. (ii) \nTenderer as a Prime Contractor (Single entity or Joint Venture member) during last 07(seven) \nyears ending as on last day of the month previous to the month of invitation of this tender, \nshould have completed/ **substantially completed Two works of *Similar nature, where the \nvalue of each work is equal to or exceed ₹ 2.37 Crores. *** \nAttachment-5 & Attachment-\n6 to Letter of Technical Bid \nOR \n3.1.A. (iii) Tenderer as a Prime Contractor (Single entity or Joint Venture member) during last 07(seven) \nyears ending as on last day of the month previous to the month of invitation of this tender, \nshould have completed/ **substantially completed Three works of *Similar nature, where the \nvalue of each work is equal to or exceed ₹ 1.90 Crores.*** \nAttachment-5 & Attachment-\n6 to Letter of Technical Bid \nOR \n3.1.A. (iv) Tenderer as a Prime Contractor (Single entity or Joint Venture member) during last 07(seven) \nyears ending as on last day of the month previous to the month of invitation of this tender, \nAttachment-5 & Attachment-\n6 to Letter of Technical Bid"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 33,
>       "text": "should have completed/ **substantially completed in any number of works of *Similar nature, \nwhere the combined value of works is equal to or exceed ₹ 4.74 Crores.*** \nNotes: \n1) \n* Similar nature work for this contract shall be, ‘Demolition/Dismantling of Buildings/Structures’. \n2) \n** Similar nature work(s) where Financial value of physically executed work is 90%  or more of the original awarded contract value or \nrevised contract value (if any), duly certified by the client, will be treated as substantially completed Similar nature work(s) and will be \nconsidered for qualification of work experience criteria. In case original contract value has been revised, the revised contract value shall \nbe considered for this purpose.  \n3) \nThe Tenderer shall furnish the Client’s Completion Certificate in support of the Work Experience criteria as specified under Clause 3.1A. \nThe certificate shall clearly indicate the Nature and Scope of Similar Nature Work, Awarded Contract Value (excluding GST), Actual \nCompletion Value (excluding GST), and the Actual Date of Completion. Where applicable, the certificate shall also specify the Revised \nContract Value (excluding GST), along with details of any variation orders and extensions of time granted. \n4) \nIn cases where the Client’s Completion Certificate indicates values inclusive of GST and/or other applicable taxes, the Tenderer shall \nsubmit the actual completion cost after deducting the applicable GST dully certified by Chartered Accountant. For portions of Similar \nNature Works executed in the pre-GST period, the Tenderer shall make a deduction towards taxes at the rate of 12% to arrive at the \ncompletion cost exclusive of taxes. \n5) \nIf a Tenderer has completed /substantially completed the Similar Nature Work(s) as a member of a Joint Venture (JV), the value of \n‘Similar Nature Work(s)’ will be recognized only in proportion to the Tenderer’s stipulated financial share in that JV. A copy of the JV \nAgreement must be submitted with the bid. \n6) \nFor the purpose of evaluation, value of works in foreign currencies shall be converted to equivalent INR as per the following procedure: \nValue of completed/ substantially completed Similar Nature Work(s) in foreign currency shall be converted to Equivalent INR by adopting \nExchange rate between the foreign currency and INR as prevailing on the date of completion / date given in the substantial completion \ncertificate as applicable, as per the website of Reserve Bank of India (RBI). \n7) \n***The value of Completed Similar Nature Work(s) executed during the previous financial years shall be updated at a rate of 5% per \nyear, compounded annually, for the Indian Rupees portion (including foreign currencies, converted to Equivalent INR), to bring them to \nthe price level of the financial year of this Tender Invitation."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 34,
>       "text": "The value of ongoing work(s) that are substantially completed shall not be updated to the price level of the financial year of this Tender \nInvitation. \n8) \nIn case the Similar Nature Work(s) are executed for client other than Government Department, PSU, or other Government Body, Contract \nAgreement, Bill wise details of payment received and Proof of TDS (Form 26AS) deducted by clients for all payments received shall be \nsubmitted duly certified by Chartered Accountant. \n9) \nWork experience certificates of the tenderers with qualification as a sub-contractor will not be considered. \n3.1. B.  FINANCIAL STANDING: The Tenderer should meet the following financial qualification criteria: \nSl. No \nCriteria Requirement \nDocument Submission \nrequirement \n3.1.B (i) \nLiquidity: Tenderer shall demonstrate that it has access to, or possess liquid assets and /or \navailability of credit facilities of not less than ₹ 1.19 Crores (Credit lines/ letter of credit/ certificates \nfrom banks for meeting the fund requirement etc.). \nAttachment-13 to Letter of \nTechnical Bid \n3.1.B (ii) \nAnnual Turnover: Tenderer in its name should have in the last five financial years (2021-2022 \nto 2025-2026) achieved in at least two financial years a minimum financial turnover (in all \nclasses of civil engineering construction works only) ≥ ₹ 9.48 Crores. **** \nAttachment-8 to Letter of \nTechnical Bid \n      Notes: \n1) \nA copy of the Banker’s Certificate issued from a Nationalised / Scheduled Bank in India (for liquidity) needs to be uploaded in the \nTechnical Proposal in respect of EQC Clause 3.1 B (i). \n2) \nAnnual financial statements (Audited Balance sheets including all related notes and income statements) for last five financial years \n(2021-2022 to 2025-2026) shall be submitted by the Tenderer. The financial information of the Tenderer must be certified either by the \nIndependent Financial Auditor (statutory Auditor) of the company appointed under the companies’ Act or by a Chartered Accountant with \nhis stamp and signature with membership number and firm registration number. \n3) \nIn case any discrepancy in data is found between the Audited Balance sheet and the financial information submitted, the data as available \nin the Audited Balance sheet will be considered."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 35,
>       "text": "4) \nIn case Audited Balance sheet of the last financial year ending with 2025-2026 is not made available by the Tenderer, he has to submit \nan affidavit certifying that 'The Balance Sheet has actually not been audited so far'. In such a case the financial data of previous '4' \naudited financial years will be taken into consideration for evaluation. If audited Balance sheet of any year other than the last financial \nyear ending with 2025-2026 is not submitted even when requested for as a clarification under clause 20 of ITT, then the Tender will be \nconsidered as non-responsive. \n5) \nAll documents shall reflect the stand-alone financial status of the Tenderer and not the Tenderer’s parent companies, subsidiaries, or \naffiliates. \n6) \nFor the purpose of evaluation, amounts in foreign currencies shall be converted to equivalent INR as per the following procedure: \nIn cases where the financial data is denominated in a currency other than Indian Rupees, it shall be converted into the equivalent value \nin Indian Rupees by adopting the exchange rate between the foreign currency and INR prevailing on the last day of the respective \nfinancial year, as published on the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) website, for the purpose of bid evaluation. \n7) \nTo align a January–December financial year with the standard April–March fiscal year, use the following conversion: \nA calendar year (e.g., January–December 2023) corresponds to the subsequent fiscal year (e.g., FY 2023–24, covering April 2023 to \nMarch 2024). Apply this rule consistently to all such conversions. \n8) \n****Financial turnover in INR/converted equivalent INR of previous financial years shall be given a weightage of 5% per year \n(compounded annually) to bring them to the price level of the Financial Year of this Tender Invitation. \n9) \nAll the documents or certifications, which are provided by Chartered Accountant after 1st July, 2019, must contain UDIN thereon and \nthe particulars of certifications must be same as mentioned on document/certification and submitted to ICAI on its website which can be \nverified online on https://udin.icai.org/search-udin. \n3.2 BID CAPACITY: \nRequirement \nDocument Submission \nRequirement \nAvailable Bid capacity should be ≥ ₹ 4.74 Crores \nFormula: \nAvailable Bid Capacity = (1.5 × A × N) – B  \nAttachment-7, Attachment-\n8 to Letter of Technical Bid"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 36,
>       "text": "Requirement \nDocument Submission \nRequirement \nWhere: \nA = Maximum value of total Civil Engineering works executed (annual turnover from Civil Engineering works) \nin any one year during the last five financial years. The turnover for previous years shall be updated by 5% \nper year (compounded annually) to bring them to the price level of the Financial Year of this Tender Invitation.  \nN = Number of years prescribed for completion of this tendered work as mentioned in NIT.  \nB = Total value (updated to the price level of the Financial Year of this Tender Invitation) of existing \ncommitments for ongoing Civil Engineering works during the completion period mentioned in NIT, from the \nlast day of the month previous to the month of invitation of this tender. \nNotes: \n1) Works in Hand information provided in ‘Attachment-7 to Letter of Technical Bid’ must be certified either by the Independent Financial \nAuditor (Statutory Auditor) of the company appointed under the companies’ Act or by a Chartered Accountant with his stamp and signature \nwith Membership Number and Firm’s Registration Number. \n2) All the documents or certifications, which are provided by Chartered Accountant after 1st July 2019, must contain UDIN thereon and the \nparticulars of certifications must be same as mentioned on document/certification and submitted to ICAI on its website, which can be \nverified online on https://udin.icai.org/search-udin."
>     }
>   ],

>   "title": "Qualification Criteria"

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n203\n          7.6      Co-contractors’ participation\n\n\n          7.6.1      In case of depot, station and other contiguous areas where more than one main\n                    contractors are working together, the Employer shall instruct the other contractors to\n                        join for the monthly SHE committee meeting of the main civil contractor, so as to\n                   discuss and decide about the common provision of security, lighting, toilet, drinking\n                   water etc. and sharing the maintenance cost of the same etc.\n\n\n          7.6.2    The general principle for sharing the cost shall be either based on the contract\n                  value of works executed at the contiguous area or the daily average number of\n               workmen employed by each contractor in the contiguous area.\n\n\n          7.7     Minimum time between two monthly SHE Committee meetings\n\n\n          7.7.1    A minimum period of 21 days shall be maintained between any two SHE monthly\n                  committee meetings.\n\n\n          7.8     Agenda\n\n\n          7.8.1    The Secretary shall circulate the agenda of the meeting at least seven working days in\n                 advance of the scheduled date of the meeting to all members.\n\n\n          7.8.2    The agenda should broadly cover the following:\n\n\n                                 i)     Confirmation of minutes\n\n                                    ii)    Chairman’s review/overview of site SHE performance / condition\n\n                                     iii)    Previous month SHE statistics\n\n                          iv)    Incident and Accident Investigation / dangerous occurrence / near miss report\n\n                      v)    Site SHE inspection\n\n                          vi)    Sub-contractors’ SHE issues\n\n                             vii)   Safety presentation by Members\n\n                               viii)  Report from Employer\n\n                          ix)   Matters arising\n\n                      x)   Any other business\n\n\n          7.9      Minutes of the meeting\n\n\n          7.9.1    The Minutes of the meeting shall be prepared as per the format provided at Form\n                   No.: SF- 002 and sent to all members within 2 working days preferably by mail/fax\n                    followed by hardcopy. Safety Committee meeting minutes shall also be displayed in\n                    the notice board for wider publicity to all concerned.\n\n\n          7.10     Disciplinary Action\n\n\n          7.10.1   The chairman shall inform the members of any outstanding issues in the meeting and\n                        in case of repeated offence/ non-compliance by some members or other co/sub\n                    contractors and propose suitable disciplinary action including provisions of monitory\n                    penalty as per the relevant contract clauses, the Employer shall ensure that the same\n                          is implemented.\n\n\n          8.0      ID Card and First day at work, SHE orientation training\n\n\n          8.1     The Contractor shall ensure that all personnel working at the site receive an induction\n             SHE training explaining the nature of the work, the hazards that may be encountered\n                    during the site work and the particular hazards attached to their own function within\n\n\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd. >       "text": "27.0     Lighting:\n\n\n27.1    The contractor shall provide sufficient site lighting, of the right type and at the right\n         place for it to be properly effective. Lighting ought not to introduce the risk of electric\n         shock. Therefore, 230V supplies should be used for those fittings, which are robustly\n           installed, and well out of reach e.g. flood lighting or high-pressure discharge lamps.\n\n27.2      Selection of Luminaries:\n\n        The contractor shall select the luminaries as per the area requirement indicated below:\n\n            Sl.   Type of         Area of Requirement             Luminaries\n         No.  Lighting\n\n             1.   Area Lighting    Workmen and vehicles       i)  Shovel type: non-symmetrical\n                                      to move about in safely.     ii)  Symmetrical or non-\n                                                             symmetrical tungsten halogen\n\n             2.  Beam flood      Concentrated light over      i)  Portable flood light (Conical\n                    lighting         an area from a            beam)\n                                        relatively great                     ii)  Wide angle flood (fan shaped\n                                    distance.                 beam)\n\n                                                                                                               iii)  Medium or narrow angle flood\n                                                                (Conical beam)\n\n             3.   Dispersive        Lighting for indoor               i)  Dispersive (Mercury florescent)\n                    lighting                                                                  ii)  Cargo cluster\n\n                                                                                                               iii)  Florescent trough\n\n             4.   Walkway lighting  Lighting for stairways,         i)  Well glass unit\n                                  ladder ways, corridors,       ii)  Bulkhead unit (tungsten filament)\n                                     scaffold access routs, etc\n                                                                                                               iii)  Bulk head unit (Florescent)\n\n             5.   Local lighting     Lighting on sites and fittin   i) PAR (Parabolic Aluminised\n                                 are generally accessible t     Reflector) lamp cluster\n                                  operatives                            ii)  Festoons (with or without shades)\n\n                                                                                                               iii)  Adjustable florescent work lamp\n\n                                                                               iv)  Portable flood lamp (mounted on\n                                                own cable drum)\n\n\n27.3    The contractor shall ensure that luminaries should always be placed so that no person\n             is required to work in their own shadow and so that the local light for one person is not"

>       "pdf_page": 51,
>       "text": "a source of glare for the others. Strongly made clamps should be available  for\n                    attaching luminaries to poles and other convenient supports.\n\n\n          27.4     Luminaries should be robust, resistant to corrosion and rain proof especially at the\n                     point of the cable entry.\n\n\n          27.5    The correct type of lamp for each luminaries should always be used and when lamps\n                need to be replaced if shall be in accordance with the supply voltage.\n\n\n          27.6    Lamp holders not fitted with a lamp should be capped off.\n\n\n          27.7    The contractor shall take every effort to illuminate the work site as per the Employer’s\n                   requirement illustrated in general instruction BMRC/SHE/CEO/0011."
>     }

### Jc61c2e8ba8123cd3

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part2#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part II: Safety|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part II: Safety]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                          224\n18.20    Requirements for Ladders\n\n\n          1)    Every contractor shall ensure that a ladder is used for work at height only if\n             a risk assessment has demonstrated that the use of more suitable work\n              equipment is not justified because of the low risk and\n\n                          i)    The short duration of use; or\n\n                            ii)    Existing features on site, which he cannot alter.\n\n          2)    Only metal ladders shall be allowed. Bamboo ladders are prohibited.\n\n          3)   Any surface upon which a ladder rests shall be stable, firm, of sufficient\n                strength and of suitable composition safely to support the ladder so that its\n              rungs or steps remain horizontal, and any loading intended to be placed on\n                            it.\n\n          4)   A ladder shall be so positioned as to ensure its stability during use\n\n          5)   A suspended ladder shall be attached in a secure manner and so that, with\n                the exception of a flexible ladder,  it cannot be displaced and swinging is\n               prevented.\n\n          6)   A portable ladder shall be prevented from slipping during use by -\n\n                          i)     securing the stiles at or near their upper or lower ends;\n\n                            ii)    an effective anti-slip or other effective stability device; or\n\n                             iii)   any other arrangement of equivalent effectiveness.\n\n          7)   A ladder used for access shall be long enough to protrude sufficiently above\n                the place of landing to which  it provides access, unless other measures\n             have been taken to ensure a firm handhold.\n\n          8)   No interlocking or extension ladder shall be used unless its sections are\n               prevented from moving relative to each other while in use.\n\n          9)   A mobile ladder shall be prevented from moving before it is stepped on.\n\n         10)  Where a ladder or run of ladders raises a vertical distance of 9 metres or\n             more above its base, there shall, where reasonably practicable, be provided\n                 at suitable intervals sufficient safe landing areas or rest platforms.\n\n         11)  Every ladder shall be used in such a way that\n\n                a)   a secure handhold and secure support are always available to the\n                      user; and\n\n                b)    the user can maintain a safe handhold when carrying a load unless, in\n                     the case of a step ladder, the maintenance of a handhold  is not\n                       practicable when a load  is  carried, and a  risk assessment has\n                   demonstrated that the use of a stepladder is justified because of\n\n                                    i)     the low risk; and\n\n                                       ii)    the short duration of use.\n\n\n19.0    Overhead protection\n\n\n            All contractors shall provide overhead protections as per Rule 41 of BOCWR\n\n                i)    Overhead protection should be erected along the periphery of every building\n              which is under construction and the building height shall be 15m or above\n                  after construction.\n\n                  ii)    Overhead protection shall be minimum 2m wide and the outer edge shall be\n           150mm higher than the inner edge and an angle not more than 200 to its\n                 horizontal sloping into the building.\n\n                  iii)   Overhead protection shall not be erected more than a height of 5m from the\n             base of the building.\n\n             iv)   Areas of inadvertent hazard of  falling of material  shall be guarded or\n               barricaded or roped-off thereby by the contractor.\n\n\n20.0     Slipping, Tripping, Cutting, Drowning and Falling Hazards\n\n        As per Rule 42 of BOCWR,\n\n                i)      All places should be free from dust, debris or similar materials.\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 36 of 114",

>       "text": "1) \nEvery contractor shall ensure that a ladder is used for work at height only if \na risk assessment has demonstrated that the use of more suitable work \nequipment is not justified because of the low risk and \ni) \nThe short duration of use; or \nii) \nExisting features on site, which he cannot alter. \n2) \nOnly metal ladders shall be allowed. Bamboo ladders are prohibited. \n3) \nAny surface upon which a ladder rests shall be stable, firm, of sufficient \nstrength and of suitable composition safely to support the ladder so that its \nrungs or steps remain horizontal, and any loading intended to be placed on \nit. \n4) \nA ladder shall be so positioned as to ensure its stability during use \n5) \nA suspended ladder shall be attached in a secure manner and so that, with \nthe exception of a flexible ladder, it cannot be displaced and swinging is \nprevented. \n6) \nA portable ladder shall be prevented from slipping during use by -  \ni) \nsecuring the stiles at or near their upper or lower ends; \nii) \nan effective anti-slip or other effective stability device; or \niii) \nany other arrangement of equivalent effectiveness. \n7) \nA ladder used for access shall be long enough to protrude sufficiently above \nthe place of landing to which it provides access, unless other measures \nhave been taken to ensure a firm handhold. \n8) \nNo interlocking or extension ladder shall be used unless its sections are \nprevented from moving relative to each other while in use. \n9) \nA mobile ladder shall be prevented from moving before it is stepped on. \n10) \nWhere a ladder or run of ladders raises a vertical distance of 9 metres or \nmore above its base, there shall, where reasonably practicable, be provided \nat suitable intervals sufficient safe landing areas or rest platforms. \n11) \nEvery ladder shall be used in such a way that \na) \na secure handhold and secure support are always available to the \nuser; and \nb) \nthe user can maintain a safe handhold when carrying a load unless, in \nthe case of a step ladder, the maintenance of a handhold is not \npracticable when a load is carried, and a risk assessment has \ndemonstrated that the use of a stepladder is justified because of \ni) \nthe low risk; and \nii) \nthe short duration of use. \n \n19.0 \nOverhead protection \n \n"

### J88c0835d94c22d50

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part2#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part II: Safety|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part II: Safety]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                          244\n           v)    Drainage shall be effectively provided and waste water shall be disposed after\n               proper treatment\n\n             vi)   Time office, canteen, drinking water,  toilet and rest place shall be suitably\n                located  for the easy access to workers.  All the  facilities shall be properly\n              cleaned and maintained during the entire period of operation.\n\n              vii)   Manual handling of cement shall be avoided to a larger extent. Whenever  it is\n                absolutely necessary the workmen shall be given  full body protection, hand\n                protection and respiratory protection as a basic measure of ensuring better\n                 health.\n\n               viii)  The PPEs provided to cement handling workmen shall conform to international\n               standards.\n\n             ix)   Access roads and internal circulation roads shall be well laid and maintained\n                properly at all time.\n\n           x)    Non-adherence to any of the above provision shall be penalised as per relevant\n                penalty clause.\n\n39.0     Personal Protective Equipments (PPEs)\n\n\n39.1    The contractor shall provide required PPEs to workmen to protect against safety and /\n          or health hazards. Primarily PPEs are required for the following protection\n\n\n                i)    Head Protection (Safety helmets)\n                  ii)    Foot Protection (Safety footwear, Gumboot, etc)\n                  iii)   Body Protection (High visibility clothing (waistcoat/jacket), Apron, etc)\n             iv)   Personal fall protection (Full body harness, Rope-grap fall arrester, etc)\n           v)   Eye Protection (Goggles, Welders glasses, etc)\n             vi)   Hand Protection (Gloves, Finger coats, etc)\n              vii)   Respiratory Protection. (Nose mask, SCBAs, etc)\n               viii)  Hearing Protection (Ear plugs, Ear muffs, etc)\n\n\n39.2    The PPEs and safety appliances provided by the contractor shall be of the\n         standard as prescribed by Bureau of Indian Standards  (BIS).  If materials\n        conforming to BIS standards are not available, the contractor as approved by\n         the Employer shall procure PPE and safety appliances.\n\n\n39.3      All construction workers should be provided with high visibility jackets with\n          reflective tapes confirming to the requirement specified under BS EN 471: 1994\n        as most of viaduct /tunnelling/station and depot works are executed either\n        above or under right-of-way. The conspicuity of workmen at all times shall be\n         increased so as to protect them from speeding vehicular traffic.\n\n39.4    The contractor shall provide safety helmet, safety shoe and high visibility clothing\n            for all employees including workmen, traffic marshal and other employees who are\n        engaged for any work under this contract as per the following requirement.\n\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 56 of 114",

>       "text": "v) \nDrainage shall be effectively provided and waste water shall be disposed after \nproper treatment \nvi) \nTime office, canteen, drinking water, toilet and rest place shall be suitably \nlocated for the easy access to workers. All the facilities shall be properly \ncleaned and maintained during the entire period of operation. \nvii) \nManual handling of cement shall be avoided to a larger extent. Whenever it is \nabsolutely necessary the workmen shall be given full body protection, hand \nprotection and respiratory protection as a basic measure of ensuring better \nhealth. \nviii) \nThe PPEs provided to cement handling workmen shall conform to international \nstandards. \nix) \nAccess roads and internal circulation roads shall be well laid and maintained \nproperly at all time. \nx) \nNon-adherence to any of the above provision shall be penalised as per relevant  \npenalty clause. \n39.0 \nPersonal Protective Equipments (PPEs) \n \n39.1 \nThe contractor shall provide required PPEs to workmen to protect against safety and / \nor health hazards. Primarily PPEs are required for the following protection \n"
>     },
>     {
>       "bbox": [
>         82.43856048583984,
>         283.4997863769531,
>         512.7667846679688,
>         765.4119873046875
>       ],
>       "block_number": 4,
>       "text": " \ni) \nHead Protection (Safety helmets) \nii) \nFoot Protection (Safety footwear, Gumboot, etc) \niii) \nBody Protection (High visibility clothing (waistcoat/jacket), Apron, etc) \niv) \nPersonal fall protection (Full body harness, Rope-grap fall arrester, etc) \nv) \nEye Protection (Goggles, Welders glasses, etc) \nvi) \nHand Protection (Gloves, Finger coats, etc) \nvii) \nRespiratory Protection. (Nose mask, SCBAs, etc)  \nviii) \nHearing Protection (Ear plugs, Ear muffs, etc) \n \n39.2 \nThe PPEs and safety appliances provided by the contractor shall be of the \nstandard as prescribed by Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS). If materials \nconforming to BIS standards are not available, the contractor as approved by \nthe Employer shall procure PPE and safety appliances. \n \n39.3 \nAll construction workers should be provided with high visibility jackets with \nreflective tapes confirming to the requirement specified under BS EN 471: 1994 \nas most of viaduct /tunnelling/station and depot works are executed either \nabove or under right-of-way. The conspicuity of workmen at all times shall be \nincreased so as to protect them from speeding vehicular traffic. \n39.4 \nThe contractor shall provide safety helmet, safety shoe and high visibility clothing \nfor all employees including workmen, traffic marshal and other employees who are \nengaged for any work under this contract as per the following requirement. \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n"
>     },
>     {
>       "bbox": [
>         546.0439453125,
>         24.479991912841797,
>         570.0199584960938,
>         41.38799285888672
>       ],
>       "block_number": 5,
>       "text": "244\n"
>     }
>   ]

### J573bee61f4ed333d

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities|Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "42.0",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 64,
>       "text": "42.0     Medical Facilities\n\n\n42.1     Medical Examination\n\n\n42.1.1   The contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his\n          sub-contractor employees employed as drivers, operators of  lifting appliances and\n          transport equipment before employing, after illness or injury,  if  it appears that the\n           illness or injury might have affected his fitness and, thereafter, once in every two years\n        up to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.\n\n\n                i)    The Contractor shall maintain the confidential records of medical examination or\n                the physician authorized by the Employer.\n\n                  ii)   No building or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination\n             and the cost of such examination is borne by contractor employing such building\n               worker.\n\n                  iii)   The medical examination shall include: -\n\n\n                a)     Full medical and occupational history.\n\n                b)     Clinical examination with particular reference to\n\n                                    i)    General Physique;\n\n                                       ii)     Vision:- Total visual performance using standard orthorator  like\n                         Titmus  Vision  Tester  should be  estimated and  suitability  for\n                         placement  ascertained  in accordance  with  the  prescribed  job\n                           standards.\n\n                                        iii)    Hearing:  - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\n                          whisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be\n                           able to hear a warning shout under noisy working conditions.\n\n                             iv)    Breathing:- Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the\n                        average peak flow rate determined out of these readings of the test\n                          performed.  The  results  recorded  at  pre-placement  medical\n                          examination could be used as a standard for the same individual at\n                           the same altitude for reference during subsequent examination.\n\n                        v)   Upper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and grip\n\n                             vi)    Spine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned.\n\n                               vii)   Lower Limbs: - Adequate leg and foot concerned.\n\n                                 viii)   General: - Mental alertness and stability with good eye, hand and\n                              foot coordination.\n\n                 c)   Any other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary\n\n\n42.1.2.     If the contractor fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above,\n          the employer will have the right to get the same conducted by through an agency with\n           intimation to the contractor and deduct the cost and overhead charges."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "text": "42.2     Occupational Health Centre\n\n          42.2.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site an occupational health centre,\n                 mobile or static is provided and maintained in good order. Services and facilities\n                as per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction medical\n                     officer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification as\n                     laid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.3    Ambulance van and room\n\n\n          42.3.1   The contractor  shall ensure  at a construction  site of a building or other\n                  construction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such\n                  construction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\n                such ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness\n                   of workers  to  hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are\n                 maintained in good repair and is equipped with standard facilities specified in\n                Schedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.4      First-aid boxes\n\n\n          42.4.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site one First-aid box for 100 workers\n                   provided and maintained for providing First-aid to the building workers. Every First-aid\n                 box  is  distinctly marked  “First-aid” and  is equipped with the  articles specified  in\n                 Schedule III of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.5     HIV/ AIDS prevention and control\n\n\n          42.5.1   The contractor shall adopt the Employer’s Policy on “HIV  / AIDS Prevention and\n                    Control for Workmen Engaged by Contractors” and the copy of the policy is given in\n                Appendix No.: 4.\n\n          42.5.2   The Employer will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid\n               down in the policy and communicate to the contractor.\n\n          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support to the appointed agency by deputing\n                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes.\n\n          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed\n                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on\n                 HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors.\n\n          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100\n                   workers) and refer them for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency\n                       for the purpose.\n\n          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point\n                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen\n                 throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly honorarium as fixed by the Employer for\n                   rendering his services in addition to his regular duty.\n\n          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained by\n                    the contractor.\n\n          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave the contractor by creating vacancy, then\n                  the contractor at his own expense train the new replacement peer educator from\n                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\n          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer\n                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor\n                   throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "text": "42.6.1   Measures shall be taken to prevent mosquito breeding at site. The measures to be\n         taken shall include:\n\n\n                i)    Empty cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water\n                  shall be deposited at a central collection point and shall be removed from the\n                   site regularly.\n\n                  ii)      Still waters shall be treated at least once every week with oil in order to prevent\n              mosquito breeding.\n\n                  iii)    Contractor’s equipment and other items on the site, which may retain water,\n                  shall be stored, covered or treated in such a manner that water could not be\n                 retained.\n\n             iv)   Water storage tanks shall be provided.\n\n\n42.6.2    Posters in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention to the dangers\n           of permitting mosquito breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site.\n\n\n42.6.3   The contractor at periodic interval shall arrange to prevent mosquito breeding\n        by fumigation  / spraying of insecticides. Most  effective insecticides  shall\n         include SOLFAC WP 10 or Baytex, The Ideal Larvicide etc.\n\n\n42.7     Alcohol and drugs\n\n\n42.7.1   The contractor shall ensure at  all times that no employee  is working under the\n          influence of alcohol / drugs which are punishable under Govt. regulations.\n\n\n42.7.2   Smoking  at  public worksites by any employee  is also prohibited as per Govt.\n          regulations."
>     }

>       "text": "          42.5.2   The Employer will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 41,
>       "text": "               down in the policy and communicate to the contractor."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 42,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 43,
>       "text": "          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support to the appointed agency by deputing"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 44,
>       "text": "                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 45,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 46,
>       "text": "          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 47,
>       "text": "                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 48,
>       "text": "                 HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 49,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 50,
>       "text": "          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 51,
>       "text": "                   workers) and refer them for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 52,
>       "text": "                       for the purpose."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 53,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 54,
>       "text": "          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 55,
>       "text": "                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 56,
>       "text": "                 throughout the contract period."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 57,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 58,
>       "text": "          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly honorarium as fixed by the Employer for"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 59,
>       "text": "                   rendering his services in addition to his regular duty."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 60,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 61,
>       "text": "          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained by"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 62,
>       "text": "                    the contractor."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 63,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 64,
>       "text": "          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave the contractor by creating vacancy, then"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 65,
>       "text": "                  the contractor at his own expense train the new replacement peer educator from"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 66,
>       "text": "                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 67,
>       "text": "          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 68,
>       "text": "                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 69,
>       "text": "                   throughout the contract period."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 70,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 71,
>       "text": "          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 72,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 73,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 74,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 65,
>       "source_line_number": 75,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 3,
>       "text": "42.6.1   Measures shall be taken to prevent mosquito breeding at site. The measures to be"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 4,
>       "text": "         taken shall include:"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 5,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 6,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 7,
>       "text": "                i)    Empty cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 8,
>       "text": "                  shall be deposited at a central collection point and shall be removed from the"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 9,
>       "text": "                   site regularly."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 10,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 11,
>       "text": "                  ii)      Still waters shall be treated at least once every week with oil in order to prevent"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 12,
>       "text": "              mosquito breeding."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 13,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 14,
>       "text": "                  iii)    Contractor’s equipment and other items on the site, which may retain water,"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 15,
>       "text": "                  shall be stored, covered or treated in such a manner that water could not be"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 16,
>       "text": "                 retained."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 17,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 18,
>       "text": "             iv)   Water storage tanks shall be provided."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 19,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 20,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 21,
>       "text": "42.6.2    Posters in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention to the dangers"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 22,
>       "text": "           of permitting mosquito breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 23,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 24,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 25,
>       "text": "42.6.3   The contractor at periodic interval shall arrange to prevent mosquito breeding"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 26,
>       "text": "        by fumigation  / spraying of insecticides. Most  effective insecticides  shall"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 27,
>       "text": "         include SOLFAC WP 10 or Baytex, The Ideal Larvicide etc."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 28,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 29,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 30,
>       "text": "42.7     Alcohol and drugs"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 31,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 32,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 33,
>       "text": "42.7.1   The contractor shall ensure at  all times that no employee  is working under the"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 34,
>       "text": "          influence of alcohol / drugs which are punishable under Govt. regulations."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 35,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 36,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 37,
>       "text": "42.7.2   Smoking  at  public worksites by any employee  is also prohibited as per Govt."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 38,
>       "text": "          regulations."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 39,
>       "text": ""
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "source_line_number": 40,
>       "text": ""
>     }
>   ],
>   "review_status": "pending_manual_review",
>   "title": "Medical Facilities"
> }

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n255\n          44.1.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site of a building or other construction\n                 work that all working areas in a free tunnel are provided with ventilation system as\n                 approved by the DG and the fresh air supply in such tunnel is not less than 6m3/min\n                       for each building worker employed underground in such tunnel and the free air flow\n                movement inside such tunnel is not less than 9m/min.\n\n\n          44.1.2   The oxygen level shall not be less than 19.5% in the working environment.\n\n\n          44.2      Illumination\n\n          44.2.1   The contractor shall take every effort to illuminate the work site as per the Employer’s\n                   requirement illustrated in general instruction BMRC/SHE/CEO/011.\n\n          44.2.2   The contractor shall conduct a monthly illumination monitoring by lux meter for\n                         all the locations and the report shall be sent to the Employer within 7th of the\n                  next month and the same shall be reviewed during the monthly SHE committee\n                  meeting.\n\n\n          45.0     Radiation\n\n\n          45.1    The use of radioactive substances and radiating apparatus shall comply with the Govt.\n                    regulatory requirements and all subsidiary legislation\n\n\n          45.2     Operations involving ionising radiation shall only be carried out after having been\n                  reviewed without objection by the Employers representative and shall be carried out in\n                  accordance with a method statement.\n\n\n          45.3     each area containing irradiated apparatus shall have warning notices and barriers, as\n                    required by the Regulations, conspicuously posted at or near the area.\n\n\n          45.4     Radioactive substances will be stored, used or disposed shall be strictly in accordance\n                     with the Govt. Enactments.\n\n\n          45.5    The contractor shall ensure that all site personnel and members of the public are not\n                 exposed to radiation.\n\n\n          46.0     Welfare measures for workers\n\n\n          46.1      Latrine and Urinal Accommodation\n\n\n          46.1.1   The contractor shall provide one latrine seat for every 20 workers up to 100\n                 workers and thereafter one for every additional 50 workers. In addition one\n                    urinal accommodation shall be provided for every 100 workers.\n\n\n          46.1.2   When women are employed, separate latrine and urinals accommodation shall be\n                   provided on the same scale as mentioned above.\n\n\n          46.1.3    Latrine and urinals shall be provided as per Section 33 of BOCWA and maintained as\n                   per Rule 243 of BOCWR and shall also comply with the requirements of public health\n                      authorities\n\n\n          46.1.4   Moving sites\n\n\n          46.1.4.1  In case of works like track laying, the zone of work is constantly moving at elevated\n                      level or at underground level. In such cases mobile toilets with proper facility to drain\n                    the sullage shall be provided at reasonably accessible distance.\n\n\n          46.1.5    In case if the contractor fail to provide required number of urinals and latrines\n                  or fail to maintain it as per the requirements of Public Health laws, the Employer\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 67 of 114",

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part5#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part V: Penalty and Awards|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part V: Penalty and Awards]]
