>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                            264\n                      PART - V: PENALTY AND AWARDS\n\n\n   56.0     Charges to be recovered from contractor for unsafe act or condition\n\n\n   56.1    BMRC has built an image of safety conscious organisation meticulously over a period\n              of seven years. Any reportable accident (fatality / injury) results in loss of life and/or\n             property damage. These accidents not only result in loss of life but also damage the\n             reputation of BMRC. Most of the accidents are avoidable and caused preliminary due\n              to contractors’ negligence. Hence BMRC shall recover the cost of damages from the\n             contractors for every reportable incident (fatality / injury).\n\n\n   56.2      In addition every BMRC work  site  is exposed to public scrutiny as the work  is\n            executed just on the right-of-way. Any unsafe act  / unsafe condition observed by\n              public further damages our reputation. Because of the non-voluntary compliance of\n             contractors to the condition of contract on SHE and project SHE manual, BMRC has\n           been forced to establish safety-enforcing organisation. The cost of established such\n             organisation is to be recovered from contractors for all observed safety violations at\n               sites.\n\n\n   56.3    The following table indicates the Safety, Health and Environment violation (unsafe act\n                      / unsafe condition) and charges to be recovered from contractors.\n\n\nSL.   TOPIC        UNSAFE ACT/UNSAFE CONDITION       DEDUCTIBLE\nNO                                              AMOUNT\n\n 1.   SHE Policy & Plan   i)  SHE policy                                 Rs.5,000   per   single\n                                                                                         violation,  compounded                        non-compliance of clause 4.1\n                                                                                     to  a  maximum   of\n                                                                     Rs.25,000 at any single\n                                                                               instance.\n\n\n                                                ii) SHE plan:                                  Rs.1,00,000 per single\n                        Not as per Employers’  content and coverage  violation, compounded\n                          (clause 4.2, 4.7)                                      to  a  maximum   of\n                                                                        Rs.2,00,000   at  any\n                        Delay in submission (clause 4.2, 4.4)\n                                                                                 single instance.\n                        Not updated as per employer’s instruction as per\n                         clause 4.4\n\n                       Copies not provided to all required supervisors  /\n                         engineers (clause 4.6)\n\n 2.   SHE Organisation  Not  complying   to  the  minimum  manpower  i)   Rs.1,00,000 per\n                         requirements as mentioned in General Instruction     month for first\n                    BMRC/SHE/CEO/ 001(clause 6.1.1)                month and\n                        Not filling up the vacancies created due to SHE     Rs.2,00,000 for\n                         personnel  leaving  the  contractor  within  14     subsequent\n                                                                    months                          days.(clause 6.7)\n                  SHE  organisation  not  provided  with  required   ii)   Rs.50,000 per\n                           Audio-visual  and  other  equipments  as  per     month for first\n                        General Instruction BMRC/SHE/CEO/03 (clause     month and\n                                                                            Rs.1,00,000 for                             6.9.2)\n                                                                        subsequent\n                       Employing  through  outsourcing  agencies  and\n                                                                    months\n                  SHE personal are not in the payroll of the main\n                           contractor (clause 6.5.1)                        For items iii), iv), v) and\n                                                                                                     vi)\n                        Disobedience  / Improper conduct  of any SHE\n                          personnel. (clause 6.2)                         Rs.50,000 for first\n                                                                                      violation and\n                          Chief SHE Manager not reporting directly to CPM\n                                                                        Rs.1,00,000 for\n                             of contractor. (clause 6.6)\n                                                                    subsequent violations\n\n\n 3.   SHE committee     Failed to formulate or conduct SHE Committee  i)   Rs.1,00,000 for the\n\n\n\n   Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 76 of 114",

>       "block_number": 3,
>       "text": " PART - V: PENALTY AND AWARDS \n \n56.0  \nCharges to be recovered from contractor for unsafe act or condition \n \n56.1  \nBMRC has built an image of safety conscious organisation meticulously over a period \nof seven years. Any reportable accident (fatality / injury) results in loss of life and/or \nproperty damage. These accidents not only result in loss of life but also damage the \nreputation of BMRC. Most of the accidents are avoidable and caused preliminary due \nto contractors’ negligence. Hence BMRC shall recover the cost of damages from the \ncontractors for every reportable incident (fatality / injury). \n \n56.2  \nIn addition every BMRC work site is exposed to public scrutiny as the work is \nexecuted just on the right-of-way. Any unsafe act / unsafe condition observed by \npublic further damages our reputation. Because of the non-voluntary compliance of \ncontractors to the condition of contract on SHE and project SHE manual, BMRC has \nbeen forced to establish safety-enforcing organisation. The cost of established such \norganisation is to be recovered from contractors for all observed safety violations at \nsites. \n \n56.3  \nThe following table indicates the Safety, Health and Environment violation (unsafe act \n/ unsafe condition) and charges to be recovered from contractors. \n \n"
>     },

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n271\n                            Canteen not provided (clause 46.2)\n\n                           Food stuff not served on no loss no profit basis\n                                 (clause 46.3)\n\n                            Creche not provided (clause 46.6)\n\n                         Non adherence of Labour welfare provisions of\n                    BOCWA (clause 3.3.1.2)\n\n                                      Fail  to  register establishment and  display the\n                                     registration   certificate   at  workplace  (clause\n                                    3.3.1.2)\n\n                            Absence of workers register and records (clause\n                                    3.3.1.2)\n\n                            Absence of muster roll and wages register (clause\n                                    3.3.1.2)\n\n                                      Fail  to  display an  abstract  of BOCWA and\n                    BOCWR (clause 3.3.1.2)\n\n         28.  Environmental     Tyre wash facility not provided (clause 47.12)      Rs.10,000 per single\n           Management       Spillage from vehicles not arrest (clause 48.9)       violation Compounded\n                                                                                            to a maximum of\n                                      Air monitoring not practiced (clause 47.17)\n                                                                            Rs.50,000 at any single\n                              Noise monitoring not practiced (clause 43.2.1)      instance\n                          The values of air monitoring and noise monitoring\n                                 not with in acceptable limits (clause 47.17, 43.2.1)\n\n                              Dust  control measures  at  sites  not  practiced\n                                 (clause 47.13)\n\n                              Improper disposal of debris / residues\n\n                         Non compliance of clause 53.0 & 54.0\n\n\n          56.4     Without limiting to the unsafe acts and or conditions mentioned above in clause 56.3\n                  the Employer shall have the right to deduct charges for any other unsafe act and or\n                  condition depending upon the gravity of the situation on a case-to-case basis. The\n                 charges shall be in comparison with that of the similar offence indicated in clause\n                     56.3.\n\n\n          57.0     Stoppage of work\n\n\n          57.1    The Employer shall have the right to stop the work at his sole discretion, if in his opinion the\n                 work is being carried out in such a way that  it may cause accidents and endanger the\n                    safety of the persons and / or property, and / or equipments. In such cases, the contractor\n                      shall be informed in writing about the nature of hazards and possible injury / accident.\n\n\n          57.2    The contractor shall not proceed with the work until he has complied with each direction to\n                    the satisfaction of Employer.\n\n          57.3    The Contractor shall not be entitled for any damages / compensation for stoppage of work,\n                due to safety reasons and the period of such stoppage of work shall not be taken as an\n                   extension of time for Completion of the Facilities and will not be the ground for waiver of\n                    levy of liquidated damages.\n\n\n          58.0    Awards\n\n                The following categories will be considered for awards as per the scheme in practice of\n                 Employer\n\n                                 i)    For every safe million man hour working without any reportable incidents\n\n                                    ii)    Zero fatality contracts\n\n                                     iii)   100% adherence to voluntary reporting of all accidents throughout the currency of\n                           contract\n\n                          iv)    Safest project team of the year.\n\n                      v)    Best SHE team of the year.\n\n                          vi)    Safest Contractor of the year.\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 83 of 114",

### J045457175f78f13f

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_appendices_instructions_forms#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Appendices, General Instructions and Forms|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Appendices, General Instructions and Forms]]

>       "pdf_page": 95,
>       "text": "BANGALORE METRO RAIL CORPORATION LTD.\n\n\n\n                                APPENDIX NO.: 4\n\n\n\n        WORKPLACE POLICY ON HIV/AIDS PREVENTION & CONTROL FOR WORKMEN\n                        ENGAGED BY CONTRACTORS\n\n             “Being mobile in and of itself is not a risk factor for HIV infection.  It is the situations\n           encountered and the behaviours possibly engaged in during mobility or migration that\n                           increase vulnerability and risk regarding HIV / AIDS.”\n\n               UNAIDS, Technical update on ‘Population, Mobility and AIDS’, February 2001, p.5\n\n        Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation (BMRC) recognizes HIV  / AIDS as a developmental\n         challenge and realizes the need to respond to  it by implementing regular HIV  / AIDS\n         prevention programmes and  creating a  non-discriminatory work environment  for HIV\n          infected workmen engaged by contractors.  For the purpose of making conscientious,\n          sensitive and compassionate decision in addressing the realities of HIV / AIDS, DMRC has\n         established these guidelines based on ILO code of practice on HIV / AIDS."
>     }

>   "text": "BANGALORE METRO RAIL CORPORATION LTD.\n\n\n\n                            General Instruction: BMRC/SHE/CEO/003\n\n\n\n         SUGGESTIVE REQUIREMENTS OF SHE MONITORING AND AUDIO-VISUAL EQUIPMENTS\n\n\n           1.    For the purpose of minimum requirements of Audio-visual and Other equipment the contracts\n                categorized into the following groups:\n\n\n\n                            Contract Value (Initial awarded value of contract)                 Group\n\n\n                                         Upto 25 Cr                            A\n\n\n                                         Upto 100 Cr                            B\n\n\n                                         Upto 250 Cr                         C\n\n\n                                   More than 250 Cr                        D\n\n\n\n           2.    Every contractor falling into the above groups shall provide the following minimum required\n               audio visual aids for conducting weekly review, monthly safety committee and other post\n               review meeting of all fatal and major incidences effectively. These audio-visual equipments\n               are a must  for  conducting  periodical  in-house  safety  presentations  in  the  training\n              programmes.\n\n           3.     In addition to the above portable hand held digital sound level meter (SLM) and portable hand\n               held digital lux meter are also to be provided.\n\n\n                                        SHE monitoring and Audio-Visual equipment\n                 Sl.   SHE monitoring and Audio-Visual                 required for\n          No          Equipment details        Group A   Group B  Group C    Group D\n                                                     Contract   Contract   Contract     Contract\n\n                 1.  Portable hand held Digital Sound Level\n                                                      1         1         1           1\n                 Meter (SLM)\n\n                 2.  Portable hand held Digital Lux Meter         1         1         1           1\n\n                 3.  Laptop Computer with standard\n                    configuration including multi media          1         1         1           1\n                         facilities\n                 4.  Colour Printer                           1         1         1           1\n\n                 5. Computer projector with screen                      -         1         1           1\n\n                 6. Overhead projector                       1\n                 7. 35mm Camera (For taking accident\n                    investigation photos in which case the        1         1         1           1\n                images can not be easily altered)\n                      Digital camera with flash of minimum 4                 8.                                       1         1         1           2\n              mega pixel and video facility\n                 9.  Digital still camera with flash of             1         2         4           6\n               minimum 4 mega pixel\n\n\n               10. Portable loudspeaker (for tool-box talk\n                                                      1         1         2           6\n                and emergency purpose)\n\n               11. Communication facility like mobile      For all supervisors and managers/engineers working\n                 phone, walky-talky etc                  Safety, Health & Environment"
> }

>       "text": "290\n                                                                 Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n\n\n\n\n\n                  BANGALORE METRO RAIL CORPORATION LTD.\n\n\n\n                   General Instruction: BMRC/SHE/CEO/008\n\n\n\n         DAYS TO BE OBSERVED FOR CREATING SHE AWARENESS\n\n\n    1st Monday to         Road Safety Week (Subjected to confirmation from Ministry of\n  Sunday of January      Road Transport, Govt. of India every year.)\n\n   16th February           Kyoto Protocol Day\n\n  March               Red Cross Month\n\n    4th March                National Safety Day\n\n    7th April                World Health Day\n\n   14th April                  Fire Safety Day\n\n   April 18 to 22            Earth Week\n\n   20th April                Earth Day\n\n   20th April               Noise Awareness Day\n\n   28th April              ILO World Day for Safety and Health at Work\n\n  May 1 to 7            Emergency Preparedness Week\n\n    5th June               World Environmental Day\n\n   12th June              World Day against Child Labours\n\n    9th July                 Occupational Health Day\n\n   17th October            World Trauma Day\n\n    1st December           World AIDS Day\n\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd. ### J52538f520778df48

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_appendices_instructions_forms#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Appendices, General Instructions and Forms|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Appendices, General Instructions and Forms]]

> {
>   "pdf_page": 107,
>   "text": "BANGALORE METRO RAIL CORPORATION LTD.\n\n\n\n                            General Instruction: BMRC/SHE/CEO/009\n\n\n\n         Minimum Requirements of SHE Communication Posters / Signages / Video\n\n\n          1.     For the purpose of Minimum requirements of SHE Communication Posters  / Signages  /\n              Video the contracts are categorized into the following groups:\n\n\n                           Contract Value (Initial awarded value of contract)              Group\n\n\n                                        Upto 25 Cr                          A\n\n\n                                       Upto 100 Cr                         B\n\n\n                                       Upto 250 Cr                       C\n\n\n                                  More than 250 Cr                      D\n\n\n          2.     Every contractor falling into the above groups shall prepare a SHE Communication Plan as a\n                 part of site specific SHE Plan and shall include the following minimum requirement of Posters /\n              Signages / Video as applicable. In case readymade posters are available in any of the category\n               from National Safety Council, Loss Prevention Association of India or any other safety related\n                organisations they may procure the same and display it. In case the same is not available then\n                the contractors’ shall make necessary arrangements to get the posters designed and printed\n             on their own.\n\n\n                    All the above are to be detailed in the Site SHE Plan and get an approval from the Employer\n                before displaying the posters.\n\n\n                                  Table No.: 1 - Minimum No. of Posters\n\n\n                                   Minimum           No. of Posters / Signage / Video\n                                          No. of\n                                              Group A  Group B  Group C  Group D\n          Sl.No    SHE Poster Title      concepts\n                                                      Contract   Contract   Contract   Contract\n                                                  in each\n                                                                 title\n\n              1.    Safety Culture                5      Each 10   Each 50   Each 75   Each 100\n\n              2.    Daily Safety Oath         1 English    Each 100  Each 200  Each 500 Each 1000\n                                   &1 Hindi\n\n              3.    Mandatory PPE Usage\n\n               a)  Signages to display the    2 types of    Each 25   Each 50   Each 75   Each 200\n               messages like PPE         sizes made\n              ZONE, NO PPE ZONE,    up of metal\n            HARD HAT AREA etc.     sheet to be\n                                      mounted at\n                                                     different\n                                                 locations\n\n              b)  Helmet                     5      Each 25   Each 50   Each 75   Each 200"
> }

> - **Schedule-A — Demolition and Dismantling**
>   - Partial & Full demolition and dismantling work as per details given in Schedule-A of the BOQ
>   - **Estimated Cost:** INR 4,69,41,306.00
> 
> - **Schedule-B — KPWD Schedule-of-Rate Items**
>   - Items under Schedule of Rate published by KPWD
>   - **Estimated Cost:** INR 5,00,000.00
> 
> - **Total of Schedules A+B:** INR 4,74,41,306.00
> 
> - **Schedule-C — Salvage Credit to BMRCL**
>   - Credit for salvageable items from dismantled/demolished private properties in which the owner has no interest
>   - **Source Reference:** Para 15 of Part-3 of Employer’s Requirement
>   - **Estimated Cost Field:** `----`
>   - **Pricing Basis:** Lump-sum credit under Preamble Instruction 3

>   "text": " BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157                                        Financial Document \n \n \n \nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                            September 2026 \nSection Page 3 of 7 \n \nPricing Document-Preamble \n1. The Pricing Document-Bill of Quantities (BOQ) & Abstract to Bill of Quantities (BOQ) \nshall be read in conjunction with the entire Tender and Contract Document including \nInstructions to Tenderers, General Conditions of Contract (including ‘Contract Data’), \nSpecial Conditions of Contract, Notice Inviting Tender, Technical Specifications, \nEmployer’s Requirements, Scope of work,  Schedule, Attachments, Appendix, Annexure, \nCorrigendum and Addendums. \n2. The quantities given in the Price document - “Bill of Quantities” are estimated \n(provisional). The basis of payment will be the actual quantities of work ordered and \ncarried out, as measured by the Contractor and verified by the Engineer and valued at \nthe rates in the accepted priced “Price Bid – Summary of Bill of Quantities”. \n3. Tenderer shall quote in the Price Bid (.xls format) sheet only in accordance with the ITT \nclause 10. Tenderer is advised to quote their percentage rate in two decimals for \nSchedule-A. Tenderer has to select option “At Par / Excess / Less” and enter percentage \nrate in figures on estimated cost to Schedule-A, for Schedule-B tenderer is required to \nselect percentage option as ‘At Par’ only and for Schedule-C tenderer shall offer Credit \nto BMRCL on Salvage of Materials, in ‘Lump sum’ amount. \n4. The Lump sum amount indicated for Schedule C will be recovered from the running \naccount bills proportionately. In case of any variation in Quantities (increase/decrease), \nthe value of credit to BMRCL will be increased/decreased according to the value of work \ndone from the ‘on account’ bills on pro-rata basis. \n5. The Contractor should note that the credit to BMRCL on salvage materials should be in \nreference to the quoted price and not on the estimate cost put to tender. \n6. The ‘Tender Price’ or ‘Quoted Price’ includes all applicable taxes, duties, cess, royalties, \nand statutory levies as per the laws in force as on last day of the month previous to the \nmonth of invitation of this tender, except Goods and Services Tax (GST). \n7. The method of measurement of completed work for payment shall be in accordance with \nthe requirements as stated in the Technical Specifications and as given in the Price \ndocument- Bill of Quantities or elsewhere in the tender documents. \n8. The quoted price is for completed and finished items of work and complete in all respects \ncomplying with the provisions of the Contract. It will be deemed to have included all \nconstructional plant, machinery tools, fuel, oil, consumables, electric power, water, \ntransportation, disposal of debris at BBMP/GBA identified C&D waste management \nfacility (dismantled/demolished material), all leads & lifts, all temporary works & false \nworks, fencing, watering, lighting, maintenance, night working and inspection facilities. \n9. The quoted price will be deemed to have included all the safety measures at work sites/ \ncasting yard, safety measures for workmen & road users, safety of the public and \nprotection of adjoining land & building while working. \n10. The quoted price will be deemed to have included establishment & overhead charges, \ncontractor’s profit, supervision, other charges together with all general risks, liabilities \nand obligations set out or implied in the contract and including remedy of any defects \nduring the Defect Liability Period.  \n11. The quoted price will be deemed to have included accommodations for staff & workmen, \nall insurance costs for labour & works, the payment of all wages, salaries, fees if any, \ncharges payable to various departments for obtaining necessary approvals if any. \n305\n",
